usage: desloppify plan [-h] [--state STATE] [--output FILE]
                       {show,queue,reset,move,describe,note,skip,unskip,reopen,done,focus,synthesize,cluster}
                       ...

Manage the living plan — a persistent layer on top of the work queue.
Track custom ordering, clusters, skips, and per-finding annotations.
Run with no subcommand to generate a full prioritized markdown plan.

positional arguments:
  {show,queue,reset,move,describe,note,skip,unskip,reopen,done,focus,synthesize,cluster}
    show                Show plan metadata summary
    queue               Compact table of upcoming queue items
    reset               Reset plan to empty
    move                Move findings in the queue
    describe            Set augmented description
    note                Set note on findings
    skip                Skip findings: temporary (default), --permanent
                        (wontfix), or --false-positive
    unskip              Bring skipped findings back to queue (reopens
                        permanent/fp in state)
    reopen              Reopen resolved findings and move back to queue
    done                Mark findings as fixed (shows score movement + next
                        step)
    focus               Set or clear active cluster focus
    synthesize          Run LLM epic synthesis to integrate review findings
                        into a meta-plan
    cluster             Manage finding clusters

options:
  -h, --help            show this help message and exit
  --state STATE         Path to state file
  --output FILE         Write to file instead of stdout

typical workflow:
  desloppify scan                       # detect findings
  desloppify plan                       # full prioritized markdown
  desloppify plan queue                 # compact table of all items
  desloppify plan cluster create ...    # group related findings
  desloppify plan focus <cluster>       # narrow scope
  desloppify next                       # work on the next item
  desloppify plan done <id> --attest .. # mark as fixed

subcommands:
  show       Show plan metadata summary
  queue      Compact table of upcoming queue items
  reset      Reset plan to empty
  move       Move findings in the queue
  done       Mark findings as fixed (score movement + next-step)
  describe   Set augmented description
  note       Set note on findings
  skip       Skip findings (temporary/permanent/false_positive)
  unskip     Bring skipped findings back to queue
  reopen     Reopen resolved findings
  focus      Set or clear active cluster focus
  cluster    Manage finding clusters
```

YOUR TASK (multi-step):
1. Run `python -m desloppify plan show` to see current clusters
2. Run `python -m desloppify plan cluster list` to see all clusters
3. Create a NEW cluster for quick wins: `python -m desloppify plan cluster create quick-ai-debt-fixes --description "Small AI-generated debt fixes that can each be done in under 5 minutes"`
4. Find some AI debt findings to add: `python -m desloppify show review --status open 2>&1 | grep ai_generated`
5. Try adding findings to your cluster: `python -m desloppify plan cluster add quick-ai-debt-fixes "<finding_id>" "<finding_id>"`
6. Show your cluster: `python -m desloppify plan cluster show quick-ai-debt-fixes`
7. Move your cluster to a strategic position: `python -m desloppify plan cluster move quick-ai-debt-fixes after delete-dead-facades`
8. Try switching focus: `python -m desloppify plan focus quick-ai-debt-fixes`
9. Run `python -m desloppify next --count 3` to see what shows up under the new focus
10. Clear focus: `python -m desloppify plan focus --clear`
11. Restore original focus: `python -m desloppify plan focus delete-dead-facades`
12. Delete your test cluster: `python -m desloppify plan cluster delete quick-ai-debt-fixes`
13. Confirm it's gone: `python -m desloppify plan cluster list 2>&1 | head -20`

```
usage: desloppify plan [-h] [--state STATE] [--output FILE]
                       {show,queue,reset,move,describe,note,skip,unskip,reopen,done,focus,synthesize,cluster}
                       ...

Manage the living plan — a persistent layer on top of the work queue.
Track custom ordering, clusters, skips, and per-finding annotations.
Run with no subcommand to generate a full prioritized markdown plan.

positional arguments:
  {show,queue,reset,move,describe,note,skip,unskip,reopen,done,focus,synthesize,cluster}
    show                Show plan metadata summary
    queue               Compact table of upcoming queue items
    reset               Reset plan to empty
    move                Move findings in the queue
    describe            Set augmented description
    note                Set note on findings
    skip                Skip findings: temporary (default), --permanent
                        (wontfix), or --false-positive
    unskip              Bring skipped findings back to queue (reopens
                        permanent/fp in state)
    reopen              Reopen resolved findings and move back to queue
    done                Mark findings as fixed (shows score movement + next
                        step)
    focus               Set or clear active cluster focus
    synthesize          Run LLM epic synthesis to integrate review findings
                        into a meta-plan
    cluster             Manage finding clusters

options:
  -h, --help            show this help message and exit
  --state STATE         Path to state file
  --output FILE         Write to file instead of stdout

typical workflow:
  desloppify scan                       # detect findings
  desloppify plan                       # full prioritized markdown
  desloppify plan queue                 # compact table of all items
  desloppify plan cluster create ...    # group related findings
  desloppify plan focus <cluster>       # narrow scope
  desloppify next                       # work on the next item
  desloppify plan done <id> --attest .. # mark as fixed

subcommands:
  show       Show plan metadata summary
  queue      Compact table of upcoming queue items
  reset      Reset plan to empty
  move       Move findings in the queue
  done       Mark findings as fixed (score movement + next-step)
  describe   Set augmented description
  note       Set note on findings
  skip       Skip findings (temporary/permanent/false_positive)
  unskip     Bring skipped findings back to queue
  reopen     Reopen resolved findings
  focus      Set or clear active cluster focus
  cluster    Manage finding clusters
```

## Report: Desloppify Plan UX Evaluation

### Current Strategy Assessment

The current strategy is **"Delete dead facades first, then migrate legacy imports, then collapse core sub-packages."** This is a well-reasoned approach:

- **delete-dead-facades** (9 items, focused): Pure deletions of zero-caller modules -- low risk, immediate score lift across abstraction_fitness, ai_generated_debt, and mid_level_elegance.
- **migrate-legacy-imports** (10 items): Depends on understanding which facades are alive before migrating callers. Correct sequencing after the delete pass.
- **core-package-consolidation** (7 items): The final structural simplification after dead code and imports are cleaned.

The strategy addresses 26 of 68 review findings and targets the biggest weighted score drags. It makes sense and is well-sequenced: delete before migrate, migrate before consolidate. The epics are clearly scoped with agent-safety annotations.

**Verdict: The strategy is sound and clearly communicated.**

### Did describe/note Commands Work?

Yes, both commands worked cleanly:

- `plan describe "<finding_id>" "text"` -- responded with `Set description on 1 finding(s).` immediately.
- `plan note "<finding_id>" "text"` -- responded with `Set note on 1 finding(s).` immediately.

No errors, no ambiguity. The finding IDs are long but copy-pasteable from the cluster show output.

### Did Annotations Appear in Cluster Show?

**Yes.** When I re-ran `plan cluster show delete-dead-facades`, the new descriptions and notes appeared inline under each finding. For example:

```
7. review::.::holistic::ai_generated_debt::state_access_pure_forwarding::bc41de9a
   File: .
   state_access.py contains 7 pure forwarding functions with docstrings that merely restate the call.
   Description: Duplicate of state_access_pure_passthrough -- same file (state_access.py), different dimension. Resolve together by deleting state_access.py.
```

And notes appeared too:

```
2. review::.::holistic::cross_module_architecture::dead_compat_shims_persist::b58ddeb4
   ...
   Note: These overlap with finding #4 (output_api_paths_api_deprecated_zero_callers). Resolve both simultaneously.
```

The rendering is clean and the annotations are visually distinct from the finding summary.

### Synthesize Command Output

The `plan synthesize` command showed:

1. All 68 open review findings listed with their dimension and ID
2. A clear instruction block at the bottom explaining the manual workflow: analyze, create clusters, add findings, set priority, focus, then mark synthesis done

**Strengths:**
- Shows all findings in one place for grouping analysis
- Provides the exact CLI commands needed to complete synthesis
- Shows that 5 epics already exist and 68 findings are new since last synthesis

**Weaknesses:**
- The output is a wall of text (68 findings listed sequentially). There is no grouping by dimension or any summary statistics to help you see patterns at a glance.
- It does not show which findings are already in clusters vs. unorganized -- you cannot tell what work remains.
- It does not suggest potential clusters based on co-occurring patterns.
- The "new since last synthesis" count (68) equals total open findings, which is misleading since the existing 5 epics already cover many of these. It seems like it is not tracking which findings were already synthesized.

### Overall Flow Intuitiveness: 7/10

The flow of `plan show` -> `plan queue` -> `next` -> `cluster show` -> `describe/note` -> `status` -> `synthesize` tells a coherent story. Each command has a clear purpose and the outputs cross-reference each other well. The cluster show output with its suggested commands at the bottom (`Resolve all`, `Drill in`, `Skip`) is particularly good for discoverability.

### Specific UX Friction Points

1. **Finding IDs are unwieldy.** IDs like `review::.::holistic::abstraction_fitness::impl_modules_zero_callers::05c5612b` are 70+ characters. For `plan describe` and `plan note`, you must type or paste these precisely. A short alias or numeric index within the cluster context would help enormously. Something like `plan describe #1 "text"` when inside a cluster context.

2. **Queue shows only 9 items despite `--top 30`.** The queue said "Queue: 9 items" and then listed 9. This makes `--top 30` feel misleading -- it seems like the 8038 skipped items include things that might be relevant. The relationship between the 76 items mentioned in `plan show` and the 9 shown in `queue` is unclear. Where did the other 67 go?

3. **`File: .` on every finding.** All 68 review findings show `File: .` which provides zero information. Holistic review findings are project-level, not file-level, so the file field is noise. It would be cleaner to suppress it when the value is `.` or show affected files instead.

4. **Synthesize does not show cluster membership.** After manually organizing findings into clusters, running `synthesize` again lists all 68 findings with no indication of which are already clustered. You cannot tell what is left to organize.

5. **No confirmation of what changed in describe/note.** The output `Set description on 1 finding(s).` does not echo the description text back, so you cannot verify it was set correctly without running `cluster show` again.

6. **Status output is very long.** The `status` command output is ~60 lines covering scores, dimensions, structural debt, workflow instructions, and agent plan. It is comprehensive but overwhelming. A `--brief` flag would help.

7. **Overlapping findings across clusters.** Items 2 and 4 in `delete-dead-facades` overlap (both mention output_api.py and paths_api.py). The tool does not flag this or suggest deduplication. When you resolve one, the other might auto-resolve, but there is no UX for this.

8. **The "note" field name.** The command is `plan note` but the cluster show renders it as `Note:`. The `next` command uses `Suggestion:` for the description. This inconsistency between `Description` in cluster show vs `Suggestion` in next output is mildly confusing -- are they the same field?

### Suggestions for Improvement

1. **Add short finding aliases** within cluster context (e.g., `plan describe delete-dead-facades:3 "text"` or positional `plan describe @3 "text"`).

2. **Show cluster membership in synthesize output.** Mark findings that are already in manual clusters with a tag like `[clustered: delete-dead-facades]` and show a summary at the top: "38 of 68 findings organized into clusters, 30 remaining."

3. **Suppress `File: .`** in display output, or replace with a list of affected files when available.

4. **Echo the annotation back** after setting it: `Set description on impl_modules_zero_callers: "Delete core/io/impl.py..."`.

5. **Add `--group-by dimension` flag to synthesize** to show findings grouped by dimension with counts, making pattern recognition easier.

6. **Clarify queue vs. total items.** The `plan show` says 76 items but queue shows 9. Add a note explaining the discrepancy (e.g., "9 of 76 in focused cluster; use `--all` to see full queue").

## Full Test Report: Cluster Lifecycle UX

### What Worked

1. **`plan show`** -- Excellent overview. Shows queue size, clusters (both manual and auto count), epics, strategy, focus, and annotations in a clean layout.
2. **`plan cluster list`** -- Clean listing with `[auto]` tags to distinguish auto-generated vs manual clusters. Good descriptions.
3. **`plan cluster create quick-ai-debt-fixes --description "..."`** -- Worked perfectly. Simple, clean confirmation message.
4. **`plan cluster add <cluster> <id> <id> <id>`** -- Worked well. Accepts multiple finding IDs in one command. Clear confirmation ("Added 3 item(s) to cluster").
5. **`plan cluster show <cluster>`** -- Excellent output. Shows type, description, numbered members with file and finding description, and helpful follow-up commands at the bottom (`Resolve all`, `Drill in`, `Skip`).
6. **`plan cluster move <cluster> after <other>`** -- Worked. Positioning syntax is intuitive.
7. **`plan focus <cluster>`** / **`plan focus --clear`** -- Both worked cleanly with simple confirmation messages.
8. **`next --count 3`** with focus -- Focus mode correctly filtered to show only the 3 items in the focused cluster. The output clearly states "Focused on: quick-ai-debt-fixes (3 of 3 remaining)" so the user knows the filter is active.

### What Didn't Work

1. **BUG: `plan cluster delete` requires two invocations.** The first `delete` prints "Deleted cluster quick-ai-debt-fixes (3 items orphaned)" but the cluster persists in both `list` and `show`. Running `delete` a second time actually removes it. This is likely a save/persistence issue -- the in-memory deletion may not be flushed to disk on the first call, or there is a reconciliation step re-creating the cluster from orphaned members before the write completes.

2. **`plan cluster move` output is vague.** It says "Moved cluster quick-ai-debt-fixes (3 items) to after." -- the word "after" is a positional keyword, not a destination name. It should say something like "Moved cluster quick-ai-debt-fixes (3 items) to position after delete-dead-facades."

### Focus Mode UX

Focus mode worked well. Key observations:
- `next` correctly restricted output to only the focused cluster's items.
- The header "Focused on: quick-ai-debt-fixes (3 of 3 remaining)" is clear.
- Clearing and restoring focus was straightforward.
- Tier Navigator still shows within the focused context (T1:3 T2:0 T3:0 T4:0), which makes sense.
- The meta-plan/strategy text still appears even in focused mode, which provides useful strategic context.

### Intuitiveness Rating: 7/10

The workflow is largely intuitive, with a natural CRUD feel. The subcommand nesting (`plan cluster create`, `plan cluster add`, etc.) is consistent and discoverable. Docking points for the delete bug, the vague move output, and a few minor friction points listed below.

### Specific UX Friction Points

1. **Delete bug (critical):** Must run delete twice. This would confuse any user who checks afterward. The first run lies about success.

2. **`cluster move` output lacks destination context.** "to after" is confusing. Should echo the reference cluster name.

3. **Finding IDs are extremely long.** `review::.::holistic::ai_generated_debt::coerce_target_score_defensive_fallback_check::44e72d1f` is 86 characters. Having to copy-paste multiple of these into `cluster add` is tedious. A short-hash or numeric alias (like the `[#76]` shown in `next` output) would dramatically improve ergonomics.

4. **No `cluster remove` to remove a single finding from a cluster.** There's `add` but no inverse. If you add the wrong finding, you'd presumably have to delete the cluster and recreate it. (Unless there's an unlisted command I didn't test.)

5. **`show review --status open` was the right discovery path, but there's no `--dimension` filter.** To find AI debt findings I had to grep the output. A `--dimension ai_generated_debt` flag would make finding candidates easier.

6. **Auto-clusters clutter the `cluster list` output.** 57 auto-clusters mixed with 8 manual ones makes scanning hard. A `--manual` / `--auto` filter, or at least grouping them visually (manual first, auto second), would help. Currently auto-clusters appear first in the list, burying the manual ones at the bottom.

### Missing Commands I Wished Existed

1. **`plan cluster remove <cluster> <finding_id>`** -- Remove a single finding from a cluster without deleting the whole cluster.
2. **`plan cluster list --manual`** -- Filter to only show manually-created clusters.
3. **`plan cluster rename <old> <new>`** -- Rename a cluster without recreating it.
4. **Short finding aliases in `cluster add`** -- Accept `#76` or the 8-char hash suffix (`44e72d1f`) instead of the full finding ID.
5. **`plan cluster merge <source> <target>`** -- Merge one cluster's findings into another.

## Desloppify Plan UX Report: Fix-and-Resolve Workflow Testing

### 1. "What to fix" guidance from `next` -- Rating: 8/10

The `next` command provides genuinely actionable guidance. For the top-priority item (`impl_modules_zero_callers`), the output includes:

- **What's wrong**: "core/io/impl.py, core/pathing/impl.py, and core/discovery/impl.py are star-import shims with zero importers."
- **What to do**: "Delete core/io/impl.py, core/pathing/impl.py, core/discovery/impl.py -- star-import shims with zero importers. Safe to delete."
- **Cluster context**: Part of `delete-dead-facades` cluster (9 items)
- **Confidence level**: Tier 1, high confidence
- **Exact resolve command**: Copy-pasteable `plan done` with attestation template

**Strengths**: The suggestion line ("Delete all three dead modules") is concise and direct. The related-files list in `show` output helps locate the actual code. The meta-plan at the top provides strategic framing.

**Weaknesses**: The `File: .` field is confusing -- it shows `.` (codebase root) instead of the actual files involved. This is because holistic review findings are project-level, but the UX doesn't explain that. The related files are only visible in the `show` output, not in `next`. For an agent or human doing the work, having to run a separate `show` command to get file paths is friction.

### 2. Attestation Requirement -- Clear and Helpful

The attestation system works well as an anti-gaming mechanism:

- **Without `--attest`**: Clear rejection message explaining exactly what keywords are required (`'I have actually'` and `'not gaming'`).
- **With bad attestation (`--attest "yes"`)**: Specific feedback about which keywords are missing, with a full example template.
- The template is well-designed: `"I have actually [DESCRIBE THE CONCRETE CHANGE YOU MADE] and I am not gaming the score by resolving without fixing."` -- this forces the agent/user to describe what they actually did.

**Verdict**: Helpful, not annoying. The rejection messages are informative, not just error codes. The forced natural-language description of the change is a clever anti-gaming mechanism. The only minor friction is that the attestation string is long and somewhat awkward to type for a human (but agents will paste it).

### 3. Skip/Unskip Workflow -- Functional but with a Bug

**Skip**: Worked cleanly. `plan skip` produced a clear confirmation ("Skipped 1 item(s)") and a useful tip about running `plan synthesize`.

**Queue advancement after skip**: The queue correctly advanced to the next cluster (`quick-ai-debt-fixes`). However, this was surprising -- skipping one item from the focused cluster (`delete-dead-facades`) caused the focus to shift entirely, because the tier-filtered view only showed 1 item from that cluster (the rest were apparently filtered at a different tier in that view). After re-running `next` without the focus filter, only 3 T1 items remained visible.

**Unskip**: Worked (`"Unskipped 1 item(s) -- back in queue"`), but revealed a significant UX issue:
- **The unskipped item was placed at position #79 (end of queue) instead of its original position #1**. This means skip/unskip is not a true "snooze" -- it permanently demotes the item's priority. An agent that accidentally skips a top-priority item has no way to restore its position without manually running `plan move`.
- After unskip, the tier counts jumped dramatically (T1:8 to T1:63, T2:0 to T2:192). This suggests the unskip triggered a queue recalculation that rehydrated items from the skip pool, which is confusing and unexpected.

### 4. `plan done` vs `resolve` -- Makes Sense

The `plan done` command is the plan-aware version of resolve. The distinction is clear:
- `plan done` marks a finding as resolved within the living plan context, with attestation
- `plan skip --permanent` is the "won't fix" equivalent, also requiring attestation
- Temporary `plan skip` (no `--permanent`) is for deferral

The resolve-with block in `next` output provides both options side by side, which is helpful. The copy-pasteable commands with placeholders (`<what you did>`, `<why>`) are a nice touch.

### 5. Tier/Cluster Filtering in `next` -- Rating: 7/10

**Tier filtering**: When I requested `--tier 2` with `--count 3`, it gracefully fell back: "Requested T2 has 0 open -> showing T1 (nearest non-empty)." This is a sensible default rather than showing nothing.

**Cluster filtering**: `--cluster auto/dupes-exact --count 3` worked perfectly, showing exactly 3 items from that cluster with clear formatting and impact estimates ("fixing all 82 dupes issues -> ~+0.2 pts").

**Tier Navigator**: The tier counts at the top (`Open: T1:8 T2:1 T3:0 T4:0`) with switch commands are useful. However, the counts were inconsistent between runs (T1:8 in one run, T1:63 in another) which was confusing -- likely caused by the skip/unskip state changes, but it made the UI feel unreliable.

### 6. Overall Workflow Rating: 7/10

The workflow is well-structured and generally smooth for the "read, act, resolve" loop. The biggest issues are:

1. **Queue position loss on unskip** -- This is a real bug that would frustrate users.
2. **Inconsistent tier counts** between runs (likely a state consistency issue after skip/unskip).
3. **`File: .`** for holistic findings is confusing -- it should either show "codebase-wide" or list the related files inline.
4. **Focus was lost during skip** -- When the focused cluster's items were all temporarily skipped, focus shifted silently to another cluster. A nudge like "Focus cluster delete-dead-facades has no visible items. Showing next available cluster." would help.

### 7. Specific UX Friction Points

1. **Queue position not preserved on unskip**: Item went from #1 to #79 after skip+unskip cycle. This makes skip dangerous rather than casual.

2. **`File: .` field is uninformative**: Holistic review findings show `File: .` which tells the user nothing. The related files are only visible in `show` output, requiring an extra step.

3. **Focus silently shifts**: After skipping the last visible item in the focused cluster, `next` showed a different cluster without explanation. Users may not realize their focus changed.

4. **"coercions_api" finding was T2 in the cluster but not shown in focused view**: The `delete-dead-facades` cluster has 9 items, but the focused `next` view only showed 1 (the T1 item). The other 8 items in the cluster were not visible until using `--count 9` with `--cluster delete-dead-facades`. This is because focus + tier filtering are combined, and most cluster items were filtered out.

5. **Attestation template is identical for `done` and `skip --permanent`**: The template says "I have actually [DESCRIBE THE CONCRETE CHANGE YOU MADE]" for both, but for `skip --permanent`, the user is explicitly NOT making a change. The template should be different for skips.

6. **"Tip: review findings changed -- run `desloppify plan synthesize`"**: This staleness nudge appeared after skip, which is good. However, it's only a "tip" and could be more prominent.

7. **`show` output says "85 ordered, 8042 skipped"** while `plan show` says "79 items prioritized, 8040 skipped": The counts disagree between surfaces, suggesting different counting logic. This inconsistency undermines trust in the numbers.

### 8. Epic Progress Feedback and Synthesis Staleness Nudges

**Epic visibility**: Yes, I saw epic feedback. After unskip and re-running `next`, the output showed an epic-level view for `epic/core-consolidation` with its thesis, status, findings list, and a `plan focus` command. This was actually surprising -- it appeared to be showing the epic as a navigational entry point rather than a specific actionable item. It is useful for strategic context but could confuse an agent expecting a concrete task.

**Synthesis staleness nudge**: The skip command produced "Tip: review findings changed -- run `desloppify plan synthesize` to update the meta-plan." This is a useful nudge. However, I did not see any explicit staleness warnings on the `next` or `plan show` outputs about stale dimensions (despite the footer mentioning "8 stale" dimensions). The staleness data is visible in the subjective line ("15 dimensions below target, 8 stale, 68 review findings open") but is not prominently called out.

**Last synthesis timestamp**: `plan show` displays "Last synthesis: v1 (manual @ 2026-03-01T01:33:07Z)" which is helpful for knowing when the meta-plan was last refreshed.

**Missing**: No explicit "this epic is X% complete" progress bar or percentage. The epic just shows "pending" status. It would be more motivating to show "3 of 9 resolved (33%)" or similar progress tracking.

### Summary Table

| Dimension | Rating | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| "What to fix" clarity | 8/10 | Actionable, but `File: .` is confusing |
| Attestation UX | Good | Clear rejections, clever anti-gaming |
| Skip/unskip | 5/10 | Works, but loses queue position on unskip |
| `plan done` vs `resolve` | Clear | Two options presented side-by-side |
| Tier/cluster filtering | 7/10 | Graceful fallback, but inconsistent counts |
| Epic/synthesis feedback | 6/10 | Present but lacking progress % |
| **Overall workflow** | **7/10** | Solid foundation, needs polish on state management |

Here is the full report:

## What Worked

1. **Glob-style patterns for skip/unskip** -- Once I discovered that `*pattern*` glob syntax works, skip and unskip were reliable and predictable. Examples that worked:
   - `python -m desloppify plan skip "*dead_function*"` -- matched 2 items
   - `python -m desloppify plan skip "*hardcoded_url*"` -- matched 1 item
   - `python -m desloppify plan skip "*coercions*"` -- matched 2 items

2. **Unskip (temporary)** -- `python -m desloppify plan unskip "*dead_function*"` correctly brought 2 items back to queue, with clear confirmation: "Unskipped 2 item(s) -- back in queue."

3. **Unskip (permanent/wontfix)** -- `python -m desloppify plan unskip "*print_failures_and_exit*"` also worked, and helpfully indicated it reopened the finding in state: "Reopened 1 finding(s) in state. Unskipped 1 item(s) -- back in queue."

4. **Permanent skip attestation enforcement** -- The tool correctly rejected an attestation that didn't contain the required keywords ("I have actually", "not gaming"), and gave a clear error message with an example template.

5. **Count tracking** -- The `plan show` Skipped line correctly broke down by category (temp/wontfix/fp) and updated accurately with each operation.

## What Didn't Work

1. **Plain file path patterns failed silently.** `python -m desloppify plan skip "core/coercions_api.py"` returned "No matching findings found." Same for `"coercions_api"` without glob wildcards. This was the most confusing friction point. The help text says "Finding ID(s), prefix, detector name, file path, or glob" but bare file paths did not work. Only glob patterns with `*` wildcards worked.

2. **Cluster names didn't work as patterns.** `python -m desloppify plan skip "auto/smells-dead_function"` returned "No matching findings found." You'd expect a cluster name to work as a pattern since it's displayed in the queue output.

3. **Detector subtype patterns didn't work.** `python -m desloppify plan skip "smells::dead_function"` returned no matches. The `::` syntax works in finding IDs but not as a skip filter.

4. **`--include-skipped` on queue showed nothing extra.** Despite 8038+ skipped items, `python -m desloppify plan queue --top 0 --include-skipped` showed the same 9 items as without the flag. The 8038 skipped items were not displayed at all, making it impossible to review what's been skipped from the queue view.

## Confusing Error Messages / Unexpected Behavior

1. **"No matching findings found"** -- This message gives zero guidance about what went wrong or what pattern format to use. It would be much more helpful to say something like: "No findings matched 'core/coercions_api.py'. Try a glob pattern like '*coercions*', or use a finding ID from `plan queue` or `next`."

2. **Queue count fluctuations** -- The queue count changed from 76 (initial) to 79, then to 85, then 87, then 88 across operations. Some of this is from reconciliation happening in the background, but it made it hard to verify that exactly N items were skipped. The total count was not stable between operations.

3. **Focus auto-changed after skip** -- After skipping the coercions items, the focus silently changed from `delete-dead-facades` to `quick-ai-debt-fixes`, and a new cluster was created. This was unexpected -- I was just skipping items, not expecting the focus to shift or new clusters to appear.

4. **Queue doesn't show finding IDs** -- The queue table shows rank, tier, detector, summary (truncated), and cluster. But it does NOT show the finding ID. To get a finding ID you have to run `next`, which only shows one item at a time. This creates a mismatch: you can see items in the queue but can't easily get the ID needed to skip them.

## Ratings

**How easy was it to identify what to skip from the queue view? 6/10**

The queue view gives good tier/detector/summary info, and the cluster names help group things. The truncated summaries are sometimes sufficient, sometimes not (many "Needs decomposition" entries all look the same). Key issues:
- No finding IDs shown, so you can't directly use queue output as input to skip
- Summaries are truncated; no way to expand them inline (no `--verbose` flag)
- No score impact estimate per item, so "will this move the score" is pure guesswork
- The T3/T4 items with count=1 are clearly skippable, but T1 items are harder to assess

**How easy was the skip workflow itself? 5/10**

The core mechanic works, but there are significant friction points:
- **Pattern discovery is trial-and-error.** I had to try 4 different pattern formats before finding one that worked. The help text says "file path" works, but it didn't.
- **No dry-run mode.** `python -m desloppify plan skip "*coercions*"` matched 2 items, but I expected 1. There's no `--dry-run` to preview what would be matched before committing.
- **Permanent skip attestation template is rigid.** It requires specific keywords ("I have actually", "not gaming") which is good for safety but the error message could provide a fill-in-the-blank template rather than just an example.
- **Unskip is easy and intuitive.** No complaints there -- same pattern syntax, clear confirmation.

## Specific UX Friction Points

1. **No finding IDs in queue view.** This is the #1 friction point. You see items in the queue but can't reference them directly for skip/done/describe without first running `next` or guessing at glob patterns.

2. **Pattern matching is opaque.** Need documentation or `--dry-run` to understand what a pattern will match before executing.

3. **No `--verbose` / `--expand` on queue.** Truncated summaries make many items indistinguishable (e.g., 100+ "Needs decomposition" entries differ only in the truncated file path and LOC count).

4. **No score impact in queue.** It would be very useful to see estimated score impact per item or cluster, so you can make informed skip decisions. Currently you're guessing.

5. **Focus silently changed after skip.** Side effects that change the active focus should at minimum print a notice like "Focus changed from X to Y."

6. **`--include-skipped` doesn't actually show skipped items.** This flag appears to be broken or the 8038 FP items are excluded from the display even with this flag.

7. **Queue count instability.** The total queue count changes between operations (76 -> 79 -> 85 -> 87 -> 88) making it hard to reason about the effect of skip/unskip. Some kind of delta in the confirmation message (e.g., "Skipped 2 item(s). Queue: 76 -> 74") would help.

## Report: Desloppify Plan Reordering UX Test

### What Worked

1. **`plan show`** -- Excellent summary. Shows clusters, epics, strategy, focus, and queue counts in a well-organized format. This is the best command for orientation.

2. **`plan queue`** -- The compact table view is easy to scan. The `--cluster` filter works well for drilling into specific clusters. The `--top` flag with 0 for unlimited is a nice touch.

3. **`plan focus`** -- Setting and clearing focus works smoothly. When focus is active, `next` correctly narrows to only items in that cluster. The `focus --clear` UX is clean.

4. **`cluster move <name> top`** -- The command syntax is clear and the operation succeeds. The auto/unused cluster did move to position 6 (first non-epic position), which is effectively "top" given that epics are pinned.

5. **`next --tier 2`** -- Excellent for getting actionable items by severity. The auto/unused cluster showed up first here with clear "Action: desloppify fix unused-imports --dry-run" guidance.

6. **`plan move unused top`** -- Pattern-based move by detector name works well and the feedback message ("Moved 4 item(s) to top") is clear.

### What Didn't Work / Was Confusing

1. **Cluster move confirmation messages are vague**: `"Moved cluster auto/smells-broad_except (6 items) to after."` -- this should say "after auto/unused" (i.e., echo the target). Similarly, `"to up"` should say `"up 20 positions"`. The user has no confirmation of what the target was.

2. **Moves don't visibly stick in the queue view**: I ran `cluster move auto/smells-dead_function top` and `cluster move auto/review-package_organization up 20`, but the queue view showed no change in ordering. The default ranking algorithm (tier + cluster size) appears to override explicit positioning in many cases. This is the biggest friction point -- you tell the tool to move something and it says it worked, but the queue looks the same.

3. **Epics are immovable but the tool doesn't say so**: `cluster move auto/unused top` silently puts it at position 6 because epics always occupy positions 1-5. The tool should either say "Moved to position 6 (below 5 pinned epics)" or allow `--above-epics` if that's desired.

4. **Focus dramatically changes queue visibility**: With focus on `delete-dead-facades`, the queue showed 8 items. Without focus, it showed 324 entries. The skipped count also changed between invocations (8038, 8040, 8042, 8044) suggesting background reconciliation is running. This is disorienting -- the queue size and content change unpredictably between commands.

5. **Manual cluster names disappeared from queue view**: After creating manual clusters like `error-handling-cleanup` and moving them, their findings still showed under auto-cluster affiliations in the queue table. The manual cluster name only appeared when using `--cluster error-handling-cleanup`. This makes manual clustering feel like a metadata layer that doesn't actually organize the queue view.

6. **`next` numbering with focus is confusing**: When focused on `auto/unused`, the items were numbered #3, #4, #5 instead of #1, #2, #3. This suggests the first two items in the focused cluster are somehow already resolved/skipped, but that's not obvious to the user.

7. **`next --count 3` without focus shows epics, not actionable items**: The default `next` view surfaced 3 epics rather than individual findings. An agent receiving this would not know what file to edit. You need to either set focus or use `--tier` to get actionable results.

### Intuitiveness Rating: 5/10

The command vocabulary is well-designed (move, focus, cluster, queue, next). The `--help` output is good and the examples are useful. However, the gap between "what the tool says it did" and "what actually happened to the queue" is the core frustration. The tool feels like it has two competing ordering systems (manual overrides vs. algorithmic ranking) and the algorithmic system usually wins silently.

### Specific UX Friction Points

| Friction Point | Severity | Suggestion |
|---|---|---|
| Move confirmations don't echo the target | Medium | `"Moved X before Y"` not `"Moved X to before"` |
| Moves don't visibly change queue order | High | Either make moves authoritative or warn when algorithm will override |
| Epics are implicitly pinned to top | Medium | Document this or show "(pinned)" label |
| Queue item count fluctuates between calls | Medium | Avoid background reconciliation mid-session or show what changed |
| Manual clusters invisible in queue table | High | Show manual cluster names in the Cluster column |
| `next` without focus shows epics not tasks | Medium | Consider `next --actionable` flag or auto-skip epics |
| Focused `next` numbering doesn't start at 1 | Low | Reset numbering to 1 within focused view |

### Final Queue Top-10 (with focus cleared)

```
#     Tier  Detector      Summary                                       Cluster
1     T1    epic          [consolidate] core/ sub-packages               epic/core-consolidation
2     T1    epic          [delete] dead facade/shim modules              epic/delete-facades
3     T1    epic          [refactor] scattered duplication               epic/design-coherence
4     T1    epic          [migrate] legacy import chains                 epic/migrate-imports
5     T1    epic          [harden] type safety gaps                      epic/type-safety
6     T1-T2 unused        [4 items] Remove unused findings               auto/unused
7     T2    facade        [16 items] Fix file issues                     auto/facade-file
8     T3    orphaned      [14 items] Fix orphaned issues                 auto/orphaned
9     T3    flat_dirs     [8 items] Fix overload issues                  auto/flat_dirs-overload
10    T2    dupes         [75 items] Fix exact duplicates                auto/dupes-exact
```

The auto/unused quick-win cluster IS at position 6 (the highest achievable position below pinned epics), which partially achieves the goal. The error-handling and broad_except moves did not produce visible results in the queue ordering.

1→"""Compact queue table renderer for ``plan queue``."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import argparse
     6→
     7→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.runtime import command_runtime
     8→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import require_completed_scan
     9→from desloppify.core.output_api import colorize, print_table
    10→from desloppify.engine.plan import load_plan
    11→from desloppify.engine.work_queue import QueueBuildOptions, build_work_queue
    12→
    13→
    14→def _truncate(text: str, width: int) -> str:
    15→    if len(text) <= width:
    16→        return text
    17→    return text[: width - 1] + "\u2026"
    18→
    19→
    20→def _cluster_tier_label(item: dict) -> str:
    21→    """Compute a tier label from cluster members, e.g. 'T2' or 'T1-T3'."""
    22→    members = item.get("members", [])
    23→    if not members:
    24→        return ""
    25→    tiers = {int(m.get("effective_tier", m.get("tier", 3))) for m in members}
    26→    lo, hi = min(tiers), max(tiers)
    27→    if lo == hi:
    28→        return f"T{lo}"
    29→    return f"T{lo}-T{hi}"
    30→
    31→
    32→def cmd_plan_queue(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    33→    """Render a compact table of all upcoming queue items."""
    34→    runtime = command_runtime(args)
    35→    state = runtime.state
    36→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
    37→        return
    38→
    39→    top = getattr(args, "top", 30)
    40→    tier_filter = getattr(args, "tier", None)
    41→    cluster_filter = getattr(args, "cluster", None)
    42→    include_skipped = bool(getattr(args, "include_skipped", False))
    43→
    44→    plan = load_plan()
    45→    plan_data: dict | None = None
    46→    if plan.get("queue_order") or plan.get("overrides") or plan.get("clusters"):
    47→        plan_data = plan
    48→
    49→    # Auto-scope to focus cluster
    50→    effective_cluster = cluster_filter
    51→    if plan_data and not cluster_filter:
    52→        active_cluster = plan_data.get("active_cluster")
    53→        if active_cluster:
    54→            effective_cluster = active_cluster
    55→
    56→    queue = build_work_queue(
    57→        state,
    58→        options=QueueBuildOptions(
    59→            tier=tier_filter,
    60→            count=None,
    61→            scan_path=state.get("scan_path"),
    62→            status="open",
    63→            include_subjective=True,
    64→            plan=plan_data,
    65→            include_skipped=include_skipped,
    66→            cluster=effective_cluster,
    67→            collapse_clusters=True,
    68→        ),
    69→    )
    70→    items = queue.get("items", [])
    71→    tier_counts = queue.get("tier_counts", {})
    72→
    73→    # Header
    74→    total = len(items)
    75→    skipped_count = sum(1 for it in items if it.get("plan_skipped"))
    76→    non_skipped = total - skipped_count
    77→    tc_parts = " ".join(f"T{t}:{tier_counts.get(t, 0)}" for t in (1, 2, 3, 4))
    78→    finding_total = sum(tier_counts.get(t, 0) for t in (1, 2, 3, 4))
    79→    if finding_total != non_skipped:
    80→        print(colorize(f"\n  Queue: {non_skipped} entries  ({finding_total} findings: {tc_parts})", "bold"))
    81→    else:
    82→        print(colorize(f"\n  Queue: {non_skipped} items  ({tc_parts})", "bold"))
    83→
    84→    focus = plan.get("active_cluster") if plan_data else None
    85→    if focus:
    86→        print(colorize(f"  Focus: {focus}", "cyan"))
    87→
    88→    if not include_skipped and skipped_count == 0:
    89→        # Check plan for skipped items not in queue
    90→        plan_skipped_count = len(plan.get("skipped", {})) if plan_data else 0
    91→        if plan_skipped_count:
    92→            print(colorize(
    93→                f"  ({plan_skipped_count} skipped item{'s' if plan_skipped_count != 1 else ''}"
    94→                " hidden \u2014 use --include-skipped)",
    95→                "dim",
    96→            ))
    97→
    98→    if not items:
    99→        print(colorize("\n  Queue is empty.", "green"))
   100→        return
   101→
   102→    # Determine which items to show
   103→    display_items = items
   104→    if top > 0 and len(items) > top:
   105→        display_items = items[:top]
   106→
   107→    # Build rows
   108→    headers = ["#", "Tier", "Detector", "Summary", "Cluster"]
   109→    rows: list[list[str]] = []
   110→    for idx, item in enumerate(display_items, 1):
   111→        pos = str(idx)
   112→        kind = item.get("kind", "finding")
   113→
   114→        if kind == "synthesis_needed":
   115→            tier_str = "T1"
   116→            detector = "synthesis"
   117→            detail = item.get("detail", {}) if isinstance(item.get("detail"), dict) else {}
   118→            n = detail.get("total_review_findings", 0)
   119→            summary = f"[synthesis] {n} findings need integration"
   120→            cluster_name = ""
   121→        elif kind == "epic":
   122→            tier_str = "T1"
   123→            detail = item.get("detail", {}) if isinstance(item.get("detail"), dict) else {}
   124→            direction = detail.get("direction", "")
   125→            fcount = len(detail.get("finding_ids", []))
   126→            status = detail.get("epic_status", "pending")
   127→            detector = "epic"
   128→            summary = f"[{direction}] [{fcount}] [{status}] {detail.get('thesis', '')}"
   129→            cluster_name = item.get("id", "")
   130→        elif kind == "cluster":
   131→            tier_str = _cluster_tier_label(item)
   132→            member_count = item.get("member_count", 0)
   133→            detector = item.get("detector", "cluster")
   134→            summary = f"[{member_count} items] {item.get('summary', '')}"
   135→            cluster_name = item.get("cluster_name", item.get("id", ""))
   136→        else:
   137→            tier_val = int(item.get("effective_tier", item.get("tier", 3)))
   138→            tier_str = f"T{tier_val}"
   139→            detector = item.get("detector", "")
   140→            summary = item.get("summary", "")
   141→            plan_cluster = item.get("plan_cluster")
   142→            cluster_name = plan_cluster.get("name", "") if isinstance(plan_cluster, dict) else ""
   143→
   144→        suffix = ""
   145→        if item.get("plan_skipped"):
   146→            suffix = " [skip]"
   147→
   148→        summary_display = _truncate(summary, 48) + suffix
   149→        rows.append([pos, tier_str, detector, summary_display, cluster_name])
   150→
   151→    print()
   152→    widths = [4, 4, 12, 44, 24]
   153→    print_table(headers, rows, widths=widths)
   154→
   155→    if top > 0 and len(items) > top:
   156→        remaining = len(items) - top
   157→        print(colorize(
   158→            f"\n  ... and {remaining} more (use --top 0 to show all)", "dim"
   159→        ))
   160→    print()

1→"""State filtering, ignore rules, and finding pattern matching."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import fnmatch
     6→
     7→__all__ = [
     8→    "finding_in_scan_scope",
     9→    "open_scope_breakdown",
    10→    "path_scoped_findings",
    11→    "is_ignored",
    12→    "matched_ignore_pattern",
    13→    "remove_ignored_findings",
    14→    "add_ignore",
    15→    "make_finding",
    16→]
    17→
    18→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import (
    19→    Finding,
    20→    StateModel,
    21→    ensure_state_defaults,
    22→    utc_now,
    23→    validate_state_invariants,
    24→)
    25→from desloppify.core.discovery_api import rel
    26→
    27→
    28→def path_scoped_findings(
    29→    findings: dict[str, Finding],
    30→    scan_path: str | None,
    31→) -> dict[str, Finding]:
    32→    """Filter findings to those within the given scan path."""
    33→    return {
    34→        finding_id: finding
    35→        for finding_id, finding in findings.items()
    36→        if finding_in_scan_scope(str(finding.get("file", "")), scan_path)
    37→    }
    38→
    39→
    40→def finding_in_scan_scope(file_path: str, scan_path: str | None) -> bool:
    41→    """Return True when a file path belongs to the active scan scope."""
    42→    if not scan_path or scan_path == ".":
    43→        return True
    44→    prefix = scan_path.rstrip("/") + "/"
    45→    return (
    46→        file_path.startswith(prefix)
    47→        or file_path == scan_path
    48→        or file_path == "."
    49→    )
    50→
    51→
    52→def open_scope_breakdown(
    53→    findings: dict[str, Finding],
    54→    scan_path: str | None,
    55→    *,
    56→    detector: str | None = None,
    57→) -> dict[str, int]:
    58→    """Return open-finding counts split by in-scope vs out-of-scope carryover."""
    59→    in_scope = 0
    60→    out_of_scope = 0
    61→
    62→    for finding in findings.values():
    63→        if finding.get("suppressed"):
    64→            continue
    65→        if finding.get("status") != "open":
    66→            continue
    67→        if detector is not None and finding.get("detector") != detector:
    68→            continue
    69→        file_path = str(finding.get("file", ""))
    70→        if finding_in_scan_scope(file_path, scan_path):
    71→            in_scope += 1
    72→        else:
    73→            out_of_scope += 1
    74→
    75→    return {
    76→        "in_scope": in_scope,
    77→        "out_of_scope": out_of_scope,
    78→        "global": in_scope + out_of_scope,
    79→    }
    80→
    81→
    82→def is_ignored(finding_id: str, file: str, ignore_patterns: list[str]) -> bool:
    83→    """Check if a finding matches any ignore pattern (glob, ID prefix, or file path)."""
    84→    return matched_ignore_pattern(finding_id, file, ignore_patterns) is not None
    85→
    86→
    87→def matched_ignore_pattern(
    88→    finding_id: str, file: str, ignore_patterns: list[str]
    89→) -> str | None:
    90→    """Return the ignore pattern that matched, if any."""
    91→    for pattern in ignore_patterns:
    92→        if "*" in pattern:
    93→            target = finding_id if "::" in pattern else file
    94→            if fnmatch.fnmatch(target, pattern):
    95→                return pattern
    96→            continue
    97→
    98→        if "::" in pattern:
    99→            if finding_id.startswith(pattern):
   100→                return pattern
   101→            continue
   102→
   103→        if file == pattern or file == rel(pattern):
   104→            return pattern
   105→
   106→    return None
   107→
   108→
   109→def remove_ignored_findings(state: StateModel, pattern: str) -> int:
   110→    """Suppress findings matching an ignore pattern. Returns count affected."""
   111→    ensure_state_defaults(state)
   112→    matched_ids = [
   113→        finding_id
   114→        for finding_id, finding in state["findings"].items()
   115→        if is_ignored(finding_id, finding["file"], [pattern])
   116→    ]
   117→    now = utc_now()
   118→    for finding_id in matched_ids:
   119→        finding = state["findings"][finding_id]
   120→        finding["suppressed"] = True
   121→        finding["suppressed_at"] = now
   122→        finding["suppression_pattern"] = pattern
   123→    # Deferred import to avoid circular dependency with engine._state.scoring
   124→    from desloppify.engine._state.scoring import _recompute_stats
   125→
   126→    _recompute_stats(state, scan_path=state.get("scan_path"))
   127→    validate_state_invariants(state)
   128→    return len(matched_ids)
   129→
   130→
   131→def add_ignore(state: StateModel, pattern: str) -> int:
   132→    """Add an ignore pattern and remove existing matching findings."""
   133→    ensure_state_defaults(state)
   134→    config = state.setdefault("config", {})
   135→    ignores = config.setdefault("ignore", [])
   136→    if pattern not in ignores:
   137→        ignores.append(pattern)
   138→    return remove_ignored_findings(state, pattern)
   139→
   140→
   141→def make_finding(
   142→    detector: str,
   143→    file: str,
   144→    name: str,
   145→    *,
   146→    tier: int,
   147→    confidence: str,
   148→    summary: str,
   149→    detail: dict | None = None,
   150→) -> Finding:
   151→    """Create a normalized finding dict with a stable ID."""
   152→    rfile = rel(file)
   153→    finding_id = f"{detector}::{rfile}::{name}" if name else f"{detector}::{rfile}"
   154→    now = utc_now()
   155→    return {
   156→        "id": finding_id,
   157→        "detector": detector,
   158→        "file": rfile,
   159→        "tier": tier,
   160→        "confidence": confidence,
   161→        "summary": summary,
   162→        "detail": detail or {},
   163→        "status": "open",
   164→        "note": None,
   165→        "first_seen": now,
   166→        "last_seen": now,
   167→        "resolved_at": None,
   168→        "reopen_count": 0,
   169→    }
   170→
   171→
   172→def _matches_pattern(finding_id: str, finding: dict[str, str], pattern: str) -> bool:
   173→    """Check if a finding matches by ID, glob, prefix, detector, or path."""
   174→    return (
   175→        finding_id == pattern
   176→        or ("*" in pattern and fnmatch.fnmatch(finding_id, pattern))
   177→        or ("::" in pattern and finding_id.startswith(pattern))
   178→        or (
   179→            finding.get("detector") == pattern
   180→            or finding["file"] == pattern
   181→            or finding["file"].startswith(pattern.rstrip("/") + "/")
   182→        )
   183→    )
   184→
```

1→"""Plan cluster subcommand handlers."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import argparse
     6→
     7→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.runtime import command_runtime
     8→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import require_completed_scan
     9→from desloppify.app.commands.plan._resolve import resolve_ids_from_patterns
    10→from desloppify.core.output_api import colorize
    11→from desloppify.engine.plan import (
    12→    add_to_cluster,
    13→    create_cluster,

    21→
    22→def _cmd_cluster_create(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    23→    name: str = getattr(args, "cluster_name", "")
    24→    description: str | None = getattr(args, "description", None)
    25→    action: str | None = getattr(args, "action", None)
    26→    plan = load_plan()
    27→    try:
    28→        create_cluster(plan, name, description, action=action)
    29→    except ValueError as ex:
    30→        print(colorize(f"  {ex}", "red"))
    31→        return
    32→    save_plan(plan)
    33→    print(colorize(f"  Created cluster: {name}", "green"))
    34→
    35→
    36→def _cmd_cluster_add(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    37→    state = command_runtime(args).state
    38→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
    39→        return
    40→
    41→    cluster_name: str = getattr(args, "cluster_name", "")
    42→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
    43→
    44→    plan = load_plan()
    45→    finding_ids = resolve_ids_from_patterns(state, patterns, plan=plan)
    46→    if not finding_ids:
    47→        print(colorize("  No matching findings found.", "yellow"))
    48→        return
    49→    try:
    50→        count = add_to_cluster(plan, cluster_name, finding_ids)
    51→    except ValueError as ex:
    52→        print(colorize(f"  {ex}", "red"))
    53→        return
    54→    save_plan(plan)
    55→    print(colorize(f"  Added {count} item(s) to cluster {cluster_name}.", "green"))
    56→
    57→
    58→def _cmd_cluster_remove(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    59→    state = command_runtime(args).state
    60→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
    61→        return
    62→
    63→    cluster_name: str = getattr(args, "cluster_name", "")
    64→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
    65→
    66→    plan = load_plan()
    67→    finding_ids = resolve_ids_from_patterns(state, patterns, plan=plan)
    68→    if not finding_ids:

   100→    if position in ("up", "down") and target is not None:
   101→        try:

   110→    except ValueError as ex:

   112→        return
   113→    save_plan(plan)

   115→
   116→
   117→def _print_cluster_member(
   118→    idx: int, fid: str, finding: dict | None, override: dict | None = None,
   119→) -> None:
   120→    """Print a single cluster member line with optional finding details."""
   121→    print(f"    {idx}. {fid}")
   122→    if not finding:
   123→        return
   124→    file = finding.get("file", "")
   125→    lines = finding.get("detail", {}).get("lines", [])
   126→    line_str = f" at lines: {', '.join(str(ln) for ln in lines)}" if lines else ""
   127→    if file:

   129→    summary = finding.get("summary", "")
   130→    if summary:
   131→        print(colorize(f"       {summary}", "dim"))
   132→    if override:
   133→        if override.get("description"):
   134→            print(colorize(f"       Description: {override['description']}", "cyan"))
   135→        if override.get("note"):
   136→            print(colorize(f"       Note: {override['note']}", "dim"))
   137→
   138→
   139→def _load_findings_best_effort(args: argparse.Namespace) -> dict:
   140→    """Load findings from state, returning empty dict on failure."""
   141→    rt = command_runtime(args)
   142→    return rt.state.get("findings", {})
   143→
   144→
   145→def _cmd_cluster_show(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   146→    cluster_name: str = getattr(args, "cluster_name", "")
   147→    plan = load_plan()
   148→    cluster = plan.get("clusters", {}).get(cluster_name)
   149→    if cluster is None:
   150→        print(colorize(f"  Cluster {cluster_name!r} does not exist.", "red"))
   151→        return
   152→
   153→    # Header
   154→    auto_tag = "Auto-generated" if cluster.get("auto") else "Manual"
   155→    cluster_key = cluster.get("cluster_key", "")
   156→    key_type = f" ({cluster_key.split('::', 1)[0]})" if cluster_key else ""
   157→    print(colorize(f"  Cluster: {cluster_name}", "bold"))
   158→    print(colorize(f"  Type: {auto_tag}{key_type}", "dim"))
   159→    desc = cluster.get("description") or ""
   160→    if desc:
   161→        print(colorize(f"  Description: {desc}", "dim"))
   162→    action = cluster.get("action") or ""
   163→    if action:
   164→        print(colorize(f"  Action: {action}", "dim"))
   165→
   166→    # Members
   167→    finding_ids = cluster.get("finding_ids", [])
   168→    if not finding_ids:
   169→        print(colorize("  Members: (none)", "dim"))
   170→    else:
   171→        findings = _load_findings_best_effort(args)
   172→        overrides = plan.get("overrides", {})
   173→        print(colorize(f"  Members ({len(finding_ids)}):", "dim"))
   174→        for idx, fid in enumerate(finding_ids, 1):
   175→            _print_cluster_member(idx, fid, findings.get(fid), overrides.get(fid))
   176→
   177→    # Commands
   178→    print()
   179→    print(colorize("  Commands:", "dim"))
   180→    print(colorize(f'    Resolve all:  desloppify plan done "{cluster_name}" --note "<what>" --attest "..."', "dim"))
   181→    print(colorize(f"    Drill in:     desloppify next --cluster {cluster_name} --count 10", "dim"))
   182→    print(colorize(f"    Skip:         desloppify plan skip {cluster_name}", "dim"))
   183→
   184→
   185→def _cmd_cluster_list(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   186→    plan = load_plan()
   187→    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
   188→    active = plan.get("active_cluster")
   189→    if not clusters:
   190→        print("  No clusters defined.")
   191→        return
   192→    print(colorize("  Clusters:", "bold"))
   193→    for name, cluster in clusters.items():
   194→        member_count = len(cluster.get("finding_ids", []))
   195→        desc = cluster.get("description") or ""
   196→        marker = " (focused)" if name == active else ""
   197→        desc_str = f" — {desc}" if desc else ""
   198→        auto_tag = " [auto]" if cluster.get("auto") else ""
   199→        print(f"    {name}: {member_count} items{auto_tag}{desc_str}{marker}")
   200→
   201→
   202→def cmd_cluster_dispatch(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   203→    """Route cluster subcommands."""
   204→    cluster_action = getattr(args, "cluster_action", None)
   205→    dispatch = {
   206→        "create": _cmd_cluster_create,
   207→        "add": _cmd_cluster_add,
   208→        "remove": _cmd_cluster_remove,
   209→        "delete": _cmd_cluster_delete,
   210→        "move": _cmd_cluster_move,
   211→        "show": _cmd_cluster_show,
   212→        "list": _cmd_cluster_list,
   213→    }
   214→    handler = dispatch.get(cluster_action)
   215→    if handler is None:
   216→        _cmd_cluster_list(args)
   217→        return
   218→    handler(args)
   219→
   220→
   221→__all__ = ["cmd_cluster_dispatch"]
   222→

1→"""Plan override subcommand handlers: describe, note, skip, unskip, done, reopen, focus."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import argparse
     6→import sys
     7→
     8→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.runtime import command_runtime
     9→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import require_completed_scan
    10→from desloppify.app.commands.plan._resolve import resolve_ids_from_patterns
    11→from desloppify.core.output_api import colorize
    12→from desloppify.engine.plan import (

    22→def cmd_plan_describe(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    23→    """Set augmented description on findings."""
    24→    state = command_runtime(args).state
    25→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
    26→        return
    27→
    28→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
    29→    text: str = getattr(args, "text", "")
    30→
    31→    from desloppify.engine.plan import describe_finding
    32→
    33→    plan = load_plan()
    34→    finding_ids = resolve_ids_from_patterns(state, patterns, plan=plan)
    35→    if not finding_ids:
    36→        print(colorize("  No matching findings found.", "yellow"))
    37→        return
    38→
    39→    for fid in finding_ids:
    40→        describe_finding(plan, fid, text or None)
    41→    save_plan(plan)
    42→    print(colorize(f"  Set description on {len(finding_ids)} finding(s).", "green"))
    43→
    44→
    45→def cmd_plan_note(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    46→    """Set note on findings."""
    47→    state = command_runtime(args).state
    48→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
    49→        return
    50→
    51→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
    52→    text: str | None = getattr(args, "text", None)
    53→
    54→    from desloppify.engine.plan import annotate_finding
    55→
    56→    plan = load_plan()
    57→    finding_ids = resolve_ids_from_patterns(state, patterns, plan=plan)
    58→    if not finding_ids:
    59→        print(colorize("  No matching findings found.", "yellow"))
    60→        return
    61→
    62→    for fid in finding_ids:
    63→        annotate_finding(plan, fid, text)
    64→    save_plan(plan)
    65→    print(colorize(f"  Set note on {len(finding_ids)} finding(s).", "green"))
    66→
    67→
    68→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    69→# Skip / unskip
    70→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    71→
    72→def cmd_plan_skip(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    73→    """Skip findings — unified command for temporary/permanent/false-positive."""
    74→    from desloppify.app.commands.resolve.selection import (
    75→        show_attestation_requirement,
    76→        validate_attestation,
    77→    )
    78→    from desloppify.engine.work_queue import ATTEST_EXAMPLE
    79→
    80→    state = command_runtime(args).state
    81→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
    82→        return
    83→
    84→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
    85→    reason: str | None = getattr(args, "reason", None)
    86→    review_after: int | None = getattr(args, "review_after", None)
    87→    permanent: bool = getattr(args, "permanent", False)
    88→    false_positive: bool = getattr(args, "false_positive", False)
    89→    note: str | None = getattr(args, "note", None)
    90→    attestation: str | None = getattr(args, "attest", None)
    91→
    92→    # Determine skip kind
    93→    if false_positive:
    94→        kind = "false_positive"
    95→    elif permanent:
    96→        kind = "permanent"
    97→    else:
    98→        kind = "temporary"
    99→
   100→    # Validate requirements for permanent/false_positive
   101→    if kind in ("permanent", "false_positive"):
   102→        if not validate_attestation(attestation):
   103→            show_attestation_requirement(
   104→                "Permanent skip" if kind == "permanent" else "False positive",
   105→                attestation,
   106→                ATTEST_EXAMPLE,
   107→            )
   108→            return
   109→        if kind == "permanent" and not note:
   110→            print(
   111→                colorize("  --permanent requires --note to explain the decision.", "yellow"),
   112→                file=sys.stderr,
   113→            )
   114→            return
   115→
   116→    plan = load_plan()
   117→    finding_ids = resolve_ids_from_patterns(state, patterns, plan=plan)
   118→    if not finding_ids:
   119→        print(colorize("  No matching findings found.", "yellow"))
   120→        return
   121→
   122→    # For permanent/false_positive: delegate to state layer for score impact
   123→    if kind in ("permanent", "false_positive"):
   124→        from desloppify import state as state_mod
   125→        from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import state_path
   126→
   127→        state_file = state_path(args)
   128→        state_data = state_mod.load_state(state_file)
   129→        status = "wontfix" if kind == "permanent" else "false_positive"
   130→        resolved: list[str] = []
   131→        for fid in finding_ids:
   132→            resolved.extend(
   133→                state_mod.resolve_findings(
   134→                    state_data, fid, status, note or "", attestation=attestation
   135→                )
   136→            )
   137→        if resolved:
   138→            state_mod.save_state(state_data, state_file)
   139→
   140→    scan_count = state.get("scan_count", 0)
   141→    count = skip_items(
   142→        plan,
   143→        finding_ids,
   144→        kind=kind,
   145→        reason=reason,
   146→        note=note,
   147→        attestation=attestation,
   148→        review_after=review_after,
   149→        scan_count=scan_count,
   150→    )
   151→    save_plan(plan)
   152→
   153→    label = {"temporary": "Skipped", "permanent": "Wontfixed", "false_positive": "Marked false positive"}
   154→    print(colorize(f"  {label[kind]} {count} item(s).", "green"))
   155→    if review_after:
   156→        print(colorize(f"  Will re-surface after {review_after} scan(s).", "dim"))
   157→
   158→    # Nudge about synthesis staleness for review findings
   159→    has_review = any(
   160→        state.get("findings", {}).get(fid, {}).get("detector") in ("review", "concerns")
   161→        for fid in finding_ids
   162→    )
   163→    if has_review:
   164→        from desloppify.engine._plan.stale_dimensions import SYNTHESIS_ID
   165→        if SYNTHESIS_ID not in plan.get("queue_order", []):
   166→            print(colorize(
   167→                "  Tip: review findings changed -- run "
   168→                "`desloppify plan synthesize` to update the meta-plan.",
   169→                "yellow",
   170→            ))
   171→
   172→
   173→def cmd_plan_unskip(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   174→    """Unskip findings — bring back to queue."""
   175→    state = command_runtime(args).state
   176→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
   177→        return
   178→
   179→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
   180→
   181→    plan = load_plan()
   182→    # For unskip we need to match against all statuses (skipped items may be wontfix/fp)
   183→    finding_ids = resolve_ids_from_patterns(state, patterns, plan=plan, status_filter="all")
   184→    if not finding_ids:
   185→        print(colorize("  No matching findings found.", "yellow"))
   186→        return
   187→
   188→    count, need_reopen = unskip_items(plan, finding_ids)
   189→    save_plan(plan)
   190→
   191→    # Reopen permanent/false_positive items in state
   192→    if need_reopen:
   193→        from desloppify import state as state_mod
   194→        from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import state_path
   195→
   196→        state_file = state_path(args)
   197→        state_data = state_mod.load_state(state_file)
   198→        reopened: list[str] = []
   199→        for fid in need_reopen:
   200→            reopened.extend(state_mod.resolve_findings(state_data, fid, "open"))
   201→        if reopened:
   202→            state_mod.save_state(state_data, state_file)
   203→        print(colorize(f"  Reopened {len(reopened)} finding(s) in state.", "dim"))
   204→
   205→    print(colorize(f"  Unskipped {count} item(s) — back in queue.", "green"))
   206→
   207→
   208→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   209→# Reopen
   210→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   211→
   212→def cmd_plan_reopen(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   213→    """Reopen resolved findings from plan context."""
   214→    from desloppify import state as state_mod
   215→    from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import state_path
   216→
   217→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
   218→
   219→    state_file = state_path(args)
   220→    state_data = state_mod.load_state(state_file)
   221→
   222→    reopened: list[str] = []
   223→    for pattern in patterns:
   224→        reopened.extend(
   225→            state_mod.resolve_findings(state_data, pattern, "open")
   226→        )
   227→
   228→    if not reopened:
   229→        print(colorize("  No resolved findings matching: " + " ".join(patterns), "yellow"))
   230→        return
   231→
   232→    state_mod.save_state(state_data, state_file)
   233→
   234→    # Remove from skipped if present, and ensure all reopened IDs are in queue
   235→    plan = load_plan()
   236→    skipped = plan.get("skipped", {})
   237→    count = 0
   238→    order = set(plan.get("queue_order", []))
   239→    for fid in reopened:
   240→        if fid in skipped:
   241→            skipped.pop(fid)
   242→            count += 1
   243→        if fid not in order:
   244→            plan["queue_order"].append(fid)
   245→            order.add(fid)
   246→            count += 1
   247→    if count:
   248→        save_plan(plan)
   249→
   250→    print(colorize(f"  Reopened {len(reopened)} finding(s).", "green"))
   251→    if count:
   252→        print(colorize(f"  Plan updated: items moved back to queue.", "dim"))
   253→
   254→
   255→def cmd_plan_done(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   256→    """Mark findings as fixed — delegates to cmd_resolve for rich UX."""
   257→    from desloppify.app.commands.resolve.selection import (
   258→        show_attestation_requirement,
   259→        validate_attestation,
   260→    )
   261→    from desloppify.engine.work_queue import ATTEST_EXAMPLE
   262→
   263→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
   264→    attestation: str | None = getattr(args, "attest", None)
   265→    note: str | None = getattr(args, "note", None)
   266→
   267→    # Pre-validate attestation before delegating (avoids stale hint in resolve)
   268→    if not validate_attestation(attestation):
   269→        show_attestation_requirement("Plan done", attestation, ATTEST_EXAMPLE)
   270→        return
   271→
   272→    # Build a Namespace that cmd_resolve expects
   273→    resolve_args = argparse.Namespace(
   274→        status="fixed",
   275→        patterns=patterns,
   276→        note=note,
   277→        attest=attestation,
   278→        confirm_batch_wontfix=False,
   279→        state=getattr(args, "state", None),
   280→        lang=getattr(args, "lang", None),
   281→        path=getattr(args, "path", None),
   282→        exclude=getattr(args, "exclude", None),
   283→    )
   284→
   285→    from desloppify.app.commands.resolve.cmd import cmd_resolve
   286→
   287→    cmd_resolve(resolve_args)
   288→
   289→
   290→def cmd_plan_focus(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   291→    """Set or clear the active cluster focus."""
   292→    clear_flag = getattr(args, "clear", False)
   293→    cluster_name: str | None = getattr(args, "cluster_name", None)
   294→
   295→    plan = load_plan()
   296→    if clear_flag:
   297→        clear_focus(plan)
   298→        save_plan(plan)
   299→        print(colorize("  Focus cleared.", "green"))
   300→        return
   301→
   302→    if not cluster_name:
   303→        active = plan.get("active_cluster")
   304→        if active:
   305→            print(f"  Focused on: {active}")
   306→        else:
   307→            print("  No active focus.")
   308→        return
   309→
   310→    try:
   311→        set_focus(plan, cluster_name)
   312→    except ValueError as ex:
   313→        print(colorize(f"  {ex}", "red"))
   314→        return
   315→    save_plan(plan)
   316→    print(colorize(f"  Focused on: {cluster_name}", "green"))
   317→
   318→
   319→__all__ = [
   320→    "cmd_plan_describe",
   321→    "cmd_plan_done",
   322→    "cmd_plan_focus",
   323→    "cmd_plan_note",
   324→    "cmd_plan_reopen",
   325→    "cmd_plan_skip",
   326→    "cmd_plan_unskip",
   327→]
   328→
